Title: The Displaying of an horrible secte of grosse and wicked Heretiques, naming themselues the Family of Loue, with the liues of their Authours and what doctrine they teach in corners. Newly set foorth by I. R. Wherevnto is added certeine letters sent from the same Family mainteyning their opinions, which Letters are aunswered by the same I. R.
Creator: John Rogers (I. R.), author of The Summe of Christianity; with two letters from the English Family of Love (the first signed E. R.) printed with his answers
Publisher: London: [Henry Middleton] for George Bishop ("Imprinted at London for George Bishope" on the title page), 1579 (the second, enlarged edition; the first edition of 1578 is also on archive.org)
Publication Date: 1579
Rights: Public Domain (published 1579; public domain by date)
Source: Internet Archive item bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_the-displaying-of-an-hor_rogers-john-author-of-_1579, file bim_early-english-books-1475-1640_the-displaying-of-an-hor_rogers-john-author-of-_1579_djvu.txt, fetched directly by the library/source-research thread (Era VII session, one-world agent for family-of-love-and-spiritualists-franck-niclaes), 2026-10-16; host rights statement: none on the item (no possible-copyright-status and no licence field; collections pub_early-english-books-*, bim_microfilm; an EEBO microfilm scan contributed by Internet Archive, not access-restricted).
Provenance note: Header prepended; the OCR text follows unchanged apart from long-s to s normalisation (6532 occurrences). Rights basis restated: an Elizabethan London print (host date 1579; the title page names the bookseller George Bishop and the host catalogue the printer Henry Middleton) is public domain by date; no modern editorial text is in the file.
Content note: The whole print: title page, "The Preface of the Authour to the Reader" (file line 30), the Displaying itself with its numbered sections on the Family's history (David George, Henry Nicholas, Christopher Vitel) and doctrine, then "A Letter of the Familie to I. R. written by E. R." (about line 7588), "The aunswere of I. R. to the letter of the Familie of loue written by E. R." (about line 8211), and "An other letter of the familie, conteyning a Reply to the aunswer of I. R." with Rogers's reply (about line 8577). A hostile Elizabethan witness that also preserves two letters of the English Familists in their own words. EEBO microfilm OCR of a black-letter print, sampled at five points and found readable (about 0.91 of tokens read as words) with the systematic black-letter misreads ("ꝛ" for r, "/" virgules, "2" for r in "fo2"); check quotations against the page images.
